<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For each fixed bounded measurable, nonnegative, radial interaction of finite range in three dimensions that is not zero almost everywhere, we prove a positive lower bound on the constant-orbital condensate fraction that is uniform over all sufficiently small densities and all temperatures between zero and the square of the density. The interaction and density remain fixed in the thermodynamic limit.

- **claim-uniform-condensation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every potential $v$ as above there are constants $\rho_*(v)>0$ and $c_*(v)>0$ with the following property. For every $0<\rho<\rho_*(v)$ there is $L_0(\rho,v)<\infty$ such that, for every $L\ge L_0(\rho,v)$, every integer $N\ge1$ satisfying $\rho/2\le N/L^3\le2\rho$, and every $0\le T\le\rho^2$, $$\frac{\langle u_0,\gamma^{(1)}_{N,L,T}u_0\rangle}{N} \ge c_*(v).$$ Consequently, for each such fixed density $\rho$ and fixed $0\le T\le\rho^2$, every sequence with $L\to\infty$ and $N/L^3\to\rho$ has lower limiting condensate fraction at least $c_*(v)$.
  - Explanation: The constant orbital has a positive condensate fraction throughout the admitted dilute density and temperature ranges. The lower bound depends on the fixed interaction, while the volume threshold may also depend on density.
- **claim-neumann-cell-energy** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-neumann-cell)): There are $b_0,c_v>0$, depending only on the fixed potential, such that for a cube $Q$ of side $b\geq b_0$, every $y\in Q$, and every $f\in H^1(Q)$, $$\int_Q\bigl(\|\nabla f\|^2+v(x-y)\|f\|^2\bigr)\,dx \geq c_v b^{-3}\int_Q\|f\|^2\,dx.\tag{1}\label{eq:1}$$ The same conclusion holds for fixed positive coefficients in front of the two terms, with changed constants. Consequently, for disjoint cubes $Q$ of common side $b$, kinetic energy plus nonnegative pair energy bounds below the multiplication operator $$c_v b^{-3}\sum_Q n_Q\mathbf1_{\{n_Q\geq2\}}.\tag{2}\label{eq:2}$$ Both statements apply to torus cubes smaller than the torus, and to Dirichlet states extended by zero.
  - Explanation: Kinetic energy and the fixed nonnegative interaction control mass in a Neumann cube. Pairing particles inside disjoint cubes converts this into an occupation lower bound.
- **claim-kinetic-entropy-control** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-kinetic-entropy)): If $\Omega$ is an open subset of the torus with Dirichlet kinetic energy, or the full torus with periodic kinetic energy, then for every $\alpha>0$ and every finite-energy symmetric trace-one $j$-particle state, $$\alpha\operatorname{Tr}\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^j-\Delta_i\Bigr)\sigma -TS(\sigma) \geq-Tj-C_\alpha T\|\Omega\|(V^{-1}+T^{3/2}).\tag{3}\label{eq:3}$$ In particular a state of finite kinetic energy has finite entropy.
  - Explanation: A kinetic energy allowance controls the entropy contribution on a Dirichlet region or on the periodic torus. Finite kinetic energy consequently implies finite entropy.
- **claim-particle-number-free-energy** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-increments)): Uniformly in the temperature range under consideration, $$\begin{aligned} F_N&\geq c_v\rho N,\tag{4}\label{eq:4}\\ F_{N+k}&\geq F_N+c_v\rho k\qquad(k\geq0).\tag{5}\label{eq:5} \end{aligned}$$ For every bounded $W\geq0$, with no upper bound on its size required, $$F_j^{(-W)}-F_{j-1}^{(-W)}\leq C_v\rho \qquad(1\leq j\leq4\rho V).\tag{6}\label{eq:6}$$ The constant in (6) is independent of $W$.
  - Explanation: The unmodified free energy has a positive density-scale lower bound and lower forward increments. A separate insertion estimate supplies an upper increment bound independent of the size of a bounded attractive external field.
- **claim-localization-diagonal-tilting** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-localization-tilt)): Let $p^2+q^2=1$, and suppose multiplication by $p$ maps the one-particle form domain into $H^1_0(\Omega)$ for an open region $\Omega$. For a finite-energy symmetric trace-one $J$-particle state $\sigma$, let $w_j$ be the distribution of the number of particles in the $p$ color, and let $\sigma_{\mathrm{in},j},\sigma_{\mathrm{out},j}$ be the two conditional marginal states. Then $$\mathcal F^{(-W)}(\sigma)+\operatorname{Tr} n_{\|\nabla p\|^2+\|\nabla q\|^2}\sigma \geq\sum_{j=0}^Jw_j\bigl[ \mathcal F^{(-W)}(\sigma_{\mathrm{in},j})+ \mathcal F^{(-W)}(\sigma_{\mathrm{out},j})\bigr]-TH(w). \tag{7}\label{eq:7}$$ Here $H(w)=-\sum_jw_j\log w_j$, with $0\log0=0$, is the Shannon entropy, and $H(w)\leq C+\sum_jjw_j$ and $\sum_jjw_j=\operatorname{Tr}n_{p^2}\sigma$.* *Let $\Gamma=Z_N^{-1}e^{-\beta H_{N,L}}$, let $P$ be its position law, and let $G$ be a bounded symmetric Lipschitz real function of positions. Put $g=e^G$, $M_G=\mathbb E_Pe^{2G}$ and $\sigma=g\Gamma g/M_G$. For expectation under its position law write $\mathbb E_g$. Then $$\mathcal F(\sigma)-F_N\leq \mathbb E_g\sum_i\|\nabla_iG\|^2+ T\bigl(2\mathbb E_gG-\log M_G\bigr). \tag{8}\label{eq:8}$$
  - Explanation: Spatial coloring separates regional free energies with a gradient and entropy cost. Diagonal tilting bounds the free-energy change by a gradient error and the relative entropy of the position weights.
- **claim-localized-density-moments** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-density-tilts)): Let $D_0=H_0\rho$, where $H_0$ is a sufficiently large fixed number. For $J\leq N$, $0\leq W\leq\rho$, and every finite-energy symmetric trace-one $J$-particle state, $$\mathcal F^{(-W)}(\sigma)-F_J^{(-W)} \geq(c_vD_0-C_v\rho-Cs^{-2})\mathbb E_\sigma n_{\phi_U} -C_vD_0^2\|U\|.\tag{9}\label{eq:9}$$ In the corresponding equilibrium state, $$\mathbb E n_U\leq C_v\rho\|U\|.\tag{10}\label{eq:10}$$ For the unmodified $N$-particle position law, $$\log\mathbb E_Pe^{2\lambda n_{\phi_U}} \leq C_v\lambda\rho\|U\|\qquad(0\leq\lambda\leq1). \tag{11}\label{eq:11}$$ In particular $\mathbb E_P n_Q^2\leq C m^2$ for every cell $Q$. The proof of (9) can reserve a fixed positive fraction of the inside kinetic and pair energy for a further nonnegative lower bound.
  - Explanation: Localized free-energy coercivity bounds equilibrium particle counts and an exponential moment of a softened regional count. It also controls the cell count second moment and leaves energy available for isolation estimates.
- **claim-simultaneous-occupation-tails** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-occupation-tails)): There are fixed $0<b_-<1/4$ and $b_+>4$ such that for every union $U$ of $u\geq1$ specified cells, each of the events $\{n_Q<2b_-m\text{ for every }Q\subset U\}$ and $\{n_Q>b_+m/2\text{ for every }Q\subset U\}$ has probability at most $$C e^{-cmu}.\tag{12}\label{eq:12}$$ The constants and the required large fixed $K$ depend only on $v$.
  - Explanation: Specified collections of cells cannot all have very low occupation or all have very high occupation except with exponentially small probability in their total mean count.
- **claim-endpoint-isolation-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-isolation)): Fix $0<\zeta\leq1$ and set $r=\zeta\rho^{-1/3}$ and $\lambda_0=r\sqrt\rho$. A particle is nonisolated if another particle is within torus distance $r$. For any nonempty union $S$ of coarse cells and any $k'\geq0$, $$P(\#\{\text{nonisolated particles in }S\}\geq k') \leq\exp\{C_v\lambda_0\zeta^3\rho\|S\|-\lambda_0 k'\}. \tag{13}\label{eq:13}$$ The constant $C_v$ can be chosen independently of decreasing $\zeta$; the density threshold is allowed to depend on $\zeta$.
  - Explanation: An exponential tail controls the number of particles with a nearby neighbor in a specified cell union. Decreasing the isolation parameter is permitted after adjusting the density threshold.
- **claim-single-bridge-control** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-single-bridge)): Suppose a bridge’s endpoints have torus distance at most $(D+10)s$. When $L$ is sufficiently large compared with $Rs$, $\mathrm{cut}_R$ permits only the nearest lift. Under the probability bridge $\pi_{S,E}$ for that lift, its probability is at least a constant $p_*>0$. Under the same unconditioned probability bridge $\pi_{S,E}$, the expected interaction with any fixed trajectory $\omega_j$ is at most $C_v$, and the contribution of times $[r,\delta-r]$ is at most $C_v/\sqrt r$. For any fixed collection $\omega_{\mathrm{fix}}$, $$\mathbb E_{\pi_{S,E}}\left[ \mathbf1_{\mathrm{cut}_R} \sum_{j\in\mathrm{fix}}\int_0^\delta v(\omega(t)-\omega_j(t))\,dt\right] \leq C(v,D)\bigl[ \mathcal A^-(S;\omega_{\mathrm{fix}})+ \mathcal A^+(E;\omega_{\mathrm{fix}})\bigr]. \tag{16}\label{eq:16}$$ Finally, for any fixed collection of at most $N$ paths and a uniform torus point $x$, $\mathbb E_x\mathcal A^\pm(x;\omega)\leq CK^2$. These bounds are independent of temperature, density, and volume after the stated choices; $p_*$ may be fixed after $D$.
  - Explanation: Confined nearest-lift bridges have a positive cut probability, bounded interaction expectations, and a small middle-time interaction contribution. Anchor scores control their interaction with a fixed collection of paths.
- **claim-joint-trajectory-failures** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-atypical-moment)): Fix $K$ as in the cell construction. There exist $D_{\min}$, $C_v$, and $\kappa>0$, depending on the fixed potential and on $K$, such that the following holds. Fix $D\ge D_{\min}$ and $0<\zeta\le1$. For sufficiently small $\rho>0$ and sufficiently large volume at that density, uniformly for $\rho/2\le N/V\le2\rho$ and $0<T\le\rho^2$, every nonempty coarse cell union $U$ satisfies $$\log\mathbb E_{\mathrm{cl}}\exp(J_U^{\mathrm{fail}}) \le \left[C_v\sum_{j\ge0}D_j^3e^{-\kappa D_j^2} +o_\rho(1)\right]\rho\|U\|, \qquad D_j=2^jD. \tag{17}\label{eq:17}$$ The constants $C_v,\kappa$ do not depend on $D$ or $\zeta$. The term $o_\rho(1)$ is at fixed $v,K,D,\zeta$ and is uniform in the stated particle numbers, volumes, and temperatures.
  - Explanation: A localized exponential moment controls trajectory failures under the interacting closed law. Auxiliary colors and confined heat traces retain a cost proportional to the tested region rather than the whole torus.
- **claim-lipschitz-sandwich-trace** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-operator-sandwich)): For the operators just defined, put $$W_0=2a/\delta+c_1\|\nabla a\|^2, \qquad \widetilde{\mathbb H}=\mathbb H-\sum_{i=1}^N W_0(x_i).$$ Then $$\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}X^{\beta/\delta} \le\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}e^{-\beta\widetilde{\mathbb H}}. \tag{20}\label{eq:20}$$ In fact the analogous inequality holds with every positive power $q$ on the left and time $q\delta$ on the right.
  - Explanation: A trace of a positive power of the Lipschitz sandwich is bounded by a heat trace with an explicit attractive form correction. The proof controls exterior powers before summing eigenvalue powers.
- **claim-separated-anchor-scores** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-anchor-scores)): Let $U$ be a cell union and let $\mathcal D$ be any collection of anchors mutually separated by more than $r$, all within distance $Ds$ of $U$. There is a coarse-cell cover $S$ of the $(D+R_A)s$ neighborhood of $U$, with $\|S\|\leq C(D+R_A+2)^3\|U\|$, such that, pathwise, $$\sum_{a\in\mathcal D}\mathcal A^-(a;\omega) \leq C\int_0^{\delta/2}(w(t)+r^{-3})n_S(\omega(t))\,dt. \tag{21}\label{eq:21}$$ The analogous inequality holds for the end scores after time reversal. The assertion holds also when the anchors are chosen from the paths by an arbitrary measurable rule, provided their separation and location hold for each realization. There is $B_0=C(1+K^2/\zeta^3)$ such that the exponential moment of the right side of (21), divided by $B_0$, is at most $\exp(C_v\rho\|S\|)$ under the closed measure.
  - Explanation: Separated anchors admit a packing bound on their summed interaction scores. Averaging the local particle count in time gives an exponential moment even when the anchors are selected measurably from the paths.
- **claim-specified-cell-defects** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-defective-cells)): Choose $D$ and $\varepsilon$ so that $C_v\sum_{j\geq0}D_j^3e^{-\kappa D_j^2} \leq10^{-4}b_-\varepsilon$ in (17). Then choose $\zeta>0$ sufficiently small in terms of these fixed constants, followed by $C_0>\Delta_A$ sufficiently large. For small enough density, there is $q_{\mathrm{cl}}=q_{\mathrm{cl}}(\rho)\to0$ as $\rho\downarrow0$ such that every collection $\mathcal U$ of specified cells satisfies $$P_{\mathrm{cl}}(\text{every cell of }\mathcal U \text{ is defective}) \leq q_{\mathrm{cl}}^{\|\mathcal U\|}. \tag{22}\label{eq:22}$$ The bound and the convergence of $q_{\mathrm{cl}}$ are uniform in the allowed temperatures and sufficiently large volumes. Increasing $C_0$ preserves the statement.
  - Explanation: After the cutoffs are fixed in the stated order, every specified family of defective cells has a joint probability bounded by a small factor per cell. That factor tends to zero with dilution uniformly in the admitted temperature and volume ranges.
- **claim-open-insertion-transfer** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-insertion-comparison)): For any measurable symmetric slab event $\mathcal E$, $$\mu_{ab}(\mathcal E) \leq\frac C{M^2}P_{\mathrm{cl}}(\mathcal E)^{1/4}. \tag{23}\label{eq:23}$$ In particular, the insertion mass is at most $C/M^2$ and the joint bound (22) transfers to this measure with exponent $q_{\mathrm{cl}}^{\|\mathcal U\|/4}$ and prefactor $C/M^2$. All tests in this application use the actual inner endpoints.
  - Explanation: A symmetric event under an open insertion is controlled by a fractional power of its closed-law probability and a cell-pair mass factor. The event must use the actual inner endpoints.
- **claim-skeleton-encounter-moments** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-skeleton)): For the independent skeletons just constructed, there are absolute constants $c_{\mathrm{hit}}>0$ and $C<\infty$ such that, for every $R_*\ge1$, $$\mathbb E e^{\alpha J_0}\le C,\qquad \alpha=\frac{c_{\rm hit}}{(1+R_*)^2}. \tag{24}\label{eq:24}$$ For every fixed $B>0$ there are $\xi(B)>0$ and $C(B)<\infty$ such that $$\mathbb E e^{\xi(B)S_B}\le C(B). \tag{25}\label{eq:25}$$ Both estimates are uniform in $n$ and in every admissible ordered pair.
  - Explanation: Independent skeletons with the prescribed dyadic construction have bounded exponential moments for thickened encounters and soft-distance sums, uniformly over admissible endpoint pairs and torus sizes.
- **claim-deterministic-good-cell-detours** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-detour)): Apply the rejection rules just specified to a nearest-neighbor skeleton from $a$ to $b$ and a set of bad cells in $(\mathbb Z/n\mathbb Z)^3$. If the skeleton is accepted, the procedure below produces a simple path with sup-norm steps of length one from $a$ to $b$, using only good cells. Every vertex is a skeleton vertex or lies on the exterior vertex boundary of a bad component hit by the skeleton. A component $T$ is detoured at most once and contributes at most $6\|T\|$ detour vertices. Removing any whole bad components not hit by an accepted skeleton preserves acceptance and its output path.
  - Explanation: An accepted skeleton can be adapted into a simple star path through good cells using exterior boundaries of bad components and chronological loop erasure. Deleting whole components missed by the skeleton preserves the result.
- **claim-uniform-likelihood-second-moment** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-second-moment)): After fixing the cutoffs as above, there is a constant $C_v<\infty$ such that, for all sufficiently small densities and sufficiently large volumes at each density, $$\int f^2\,\mathrm d\mu\le C_v.$$ The constant is uniform for $\rho/2\le N/V\le2\rho$ and $0<T\le\rho^2$.
  - Explanation: The likelihood ratio of the accepted open proposal has a bounded second moment independent of density, temperature, and volume after the cutoff choices. This supplies the measure-comparison input for the condensate bound.
- **claim-universal-replacement-stability** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-replacement-stability)): Fix feasible endpoint and path data as in the preceding subsection. For every replacement of $B_{\mathsf j_n},B_{\mathsf d}$ on the cuts and every original witness $C,\widehat C$, one has $$\begin{gathered} S(C),S(\widehat C)\ \subseteq S_1(B),\\ S(C)=S_0(B)\ \text{pointwise outside distance }R_*\text{ of }P,\\ S(\widehat C)=S_0(B)\ \text{pointwise outside distance }R_*\text{ of }P'. \end{gathered} \tag{30}\label{eq:30}$$ In the far cells specified by the last two lines, all individual eligibility indicators and eligible counts agree as well.
  - Explanation: Every feasible cut replacement contains the witness bad sets in the stricter baseline test. Outside the encounter neighborhoods, usability and all individual selection factors agree exactly.
- **claim-feasible-path-list-control** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-path-lists)): For every original witness and every baseline cut replacement in Lemma <a href="#lem:replacement-stability" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:replacement-stability">18</a>, the witness paths $P,P'$ satisfy $G$. For arbitrary $B$, the lists contain at most $4^{b_{\rm tot}}$ path pairs. Every listed pair satisfies $$J\le J_0+C(R_*)b_{\rm tot}. \tag{31}\label{eq:32}$$ Here $J_0$ counts encounters of the original skeletons, as in Lemma <a href="#lem:skeleton" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:skeleton">15</a>.
  - Explanation: The baseline path lists contain every feasible witness pair under every cut replacement. Their size and encounter count are controlled by the total size of bridge components and the original skeleton encounters.
- **claim-integrated-cross-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cross)): Fix two simple star cell paths from $a$ to $b$, their label tuples matching the outer $Y$-cells and having a common first label, two skeletons, and endpoint data $Y,S^C,x,z$ as in <a href="#eq:27" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:27">[eq:27]</a>. With $B$, the near occurrences, $G$, and $H_F(B)$ defined above, one has $$\begin{split} \mathcal I_{I,I'}\le{}& e^{C\|\mathsf h\|+\sigma_\rho J}\mathbf1_{\mathrm{occ}} \prod_{\substack{\text{near occurrences}\\\text{in cell }Q}} [(1-\varepsilon)n_Q(Y)]^{-1}\\ &\hspace{8mm}\cdot\int W(S^B,E^B;d\omega)\,\mathbf1_G H_F(B). \end{split}\tag{32}\label{eq:31}$$ Here $C<\infty$ is fixed and $\sigma_\rho\ge0$ tends to zero as $\rho\downarrow0$ after the cutoffs are fixed, uniformly in all permitted volumes and temperatures. A cell on both paths is counted twice in the product. The right side is zero when the occupancy condition fails.
  - Explanation: The constrained cross integral is bounded by a reverse baseline integral with the common far selection factors retained. Shared labels and near encounters account for the remaining comparison cost.

        "exact_quote": "Let $p^2+q^2=1$, and suppose multiplication by $p$ maps the one-particle form domain into $H^1_0(\\Omega)$ for an open region $\\Omega$. For a finite-energy symmetric trace-one $J$-particle state $\\sigma$, let $w_j$ be the distribution of the number of particles in the $p$ color, and let $\\sigma_{\\mathrm{in},j},\\sigma_{\\mathrm{out},j}$ be the two conditional marginal states. Then $$\\mathcal F^{(-W)}(\\sigma)+\\operatorname{Tr}\n n_{|\\nabla p|^2+|\\nabla q|^2}\\sigma\n \\geq\\sum_{j=0}^Jw_j\\bigl[\n \\mathcal F^{(-W)}(\\sigma_{\\mathrm{in},j})+\n \\mathcal F^{(-W)}(\\sigma_{\\mathrm{out},j})\\bigr]-TH(w).\n \\tag{7}\\label{eq:7}$$ Here $H(w)=-\\sum_jw_j\\log w_j$, with $0\\log0=0$, is the Shannon entropy, and $H(w)\\leq C+\\sum_jjw_j$ and $\\sum_jjw_j=\\operatorname{Tr}n_{p^2}\\sigma$.*\n\n*Let $\\Gamma=Z_N^{-1}e^{-\\beta H_{N,L}}$, let $P$ be its position law, and let $G$ be a bounded symmetric Lipschitz real function of positions. Put $g=e^G$, $M_G=\\mathbb E_Pe^{2G}$ and $\\sigma=g\\Gamma g/M_G$. For expectation under its position law write $\\mathbb E_g$. Then $$\\mathcal F(\\sigma)-F_N\\leq\n \\mathbb E_g\\sum_i|\\nabla_iG|^2+\n T\\bigl(2\\mathbb E_gG-\\log M_G\\bigr).\n \\tag{8}\\label{eq:8}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-267.4"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-occupation-tails",
        "exact_quote": "There are fixed $0<b_-<1/4$ and $b_+>4$ such that for every union $U$ of $u\\geq1$ specified cells, each of the events $\\{n_Q<2b_-m\\text{ for every }Q\\subset U\\}$ and $\\{n_Q>b_+m/2\\text{ for every }Q\\subset U\\}$ has probability at most $$C e^{-cmu}.\\tag{12}\\label{eq:12}$$ The constants and the required large fixed $K$ depend only on $v$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-isolation",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $0<\\zeta\\leq1$ and set $r=\\zeta\\rho^{-1/3}$ and $\\lambda_0=r\\sqrt\\rho$. A particle is nonisolated if another particle is within torus distance $r$.         "source_id": "PM-267.4"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-single-bridge",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose a bridge’s endpoints have torus distance at most $(D+10)s$. When $L$ is sufficiently large compared with $Rs$, $\\mathrm{cut}_R$ permits only the nearest lift. Under the probability bridge $\\pi_{S,E}$ for that lift, its probability is at least a constant $p_*>0$. Under the same unconditioned probability bridge $\\pi_{S,E}$, the expected interaction with any fixed trajectory $\\omega_j$ is at most $C_v$, and the contribution of times $[r,\\delta-r]$ is at most $C_v/\\sqrt r$. For any fixed collection $\\omega_{\\mathrm{fix}}$, $$\\mathbb E_{\\pi_{S,E}}\\left[\n \\mathbf1_{\\mathrm{cut}_R}\n \\sum_{j\\in\\mathrm{fix}}\\int_0^\\delta\n v(\\omega(t)-\\omega_j(t))\\,dt\\right]\n \\leq C(v,D)\\bigl[\n \\mathcal A^-(S;\\omega_{\\mathrm{fix}})+\n \\mathcal A^+(E;\\omega_{\\mathrm{fix}})\\bigr].\n \\tag{16}\\label{eq:16}$$ Finally, for any fixed collection of at most $N$ paths and a uniform torus point $x$, $\\mathbb E_x\\mathcal A^\\pm(x;\\omega)\\leq CK^2$. These bounds are independent of temperature, density, and volume after the stated choices; $p_*$ may be fixed after $D$.",
        "source_id": "PM-267.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-single-bridge",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose a bridge’s endpoints have torus distance at most $(D+10)s$. When $L$ is sufficiently large compared with $Rs$, $\\mathrm{cut}_R$ permits only the nearest lift. For any fixed collection $\\omega_{\\mathrm{fix}}$, $$\\mathbb E_{\\pi_{S,E}}\\left[\n \\mathbf1_{\\mathrm{cut}_R}\n \\sum_{j\\in\\mathrm{fix}}\\int_0^\\delta\n v(\\omega(t)-\\omega_j(t))\\,dt\\right]\n \\leq C(v,D)\\bigl[\n \\mathcal A^-(S;\\omega_{\\mathrm{fix}})+\n \\mathcal A^+(E;\\omega_{\\mathrm{fix}})\\bigr].\n \\tag{16}\\label{eq:16}$$ Finally, for any fixed collection of at most $N$ paths and a uniform torus point $x$, $\\mathbb E_x\\mathcal A^\\pm(x;\\omega)\\leq CK^2$. These bounds are independent of temperature, density, and volume after the stated choices; $p_*$ may be fixed after $D$.",
          "source_id": "PM-267.4"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Suppose a bridge’s endpoints have torus distance at most $(D+10)s$. When $L$ is sufficiently large compared with $Rs$, $\\mathrm{cut}_R$ permits only the nearest lift. These bounds are independent of temperature, density, and volume after the stated choices; $p_*$ may be fixed after $D$.",

        "exact_quote": "Fix $K$ as in the cell construction. There exist $D_{\\min}$, $C_v$, and $\\kappa>0$, depending on the fixed potential and on $K$, such that the following holds.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-anchor-scores",
        "exact_quote": "Let $U$ be a cell union and let $\\mathcal D$ be any collection of anchors mutually separated by more than $r$, all within distance $Ds$ of $U$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-detour",
        "exact_quote": "Apply the rejection rules just specified to a nearest-neighbor skeleton from $a$ to $b$ and a set of bad cells in $(\\mathbb Z/n\\mathbb Z)^3$. If the skeleton is accepted, the procedure below produces a simple path with sup-norm steps of length one from $a$ to $b$, using only good cells. Every vertex is a skeleton vertex or lies on the exterior vertex boundary of a bad component hit by the skeleton. A component $T$ is detoured at most once and contributes at most $6|T|$ detour vertices. Removing any whole bad components not hit by an accepted skeleton preserves acceptance and its output path.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-replacement-stability",
        "exact_quote": "Fix feasible endpoint and path data as in the preceding subsection. For every replacement of $B_{\\mathsf j_n},B_{\\mathsf d}$ on the cuts and every original witness $C,\\widehat C$, one has $$\\begin{gathered}\n        S(C),S(\\widehat C)\\ \\subseteq S_1(B),\\\\\n        S(C)=S_0(B)\\ \\text{pointwise outside distance }R_*\\text{ of }P,\\\\\n        S(\\widehat C)=S_0(B)\\ \\text{pointwise outside distance }R_*\\text{ of }P'.\n\\end{gathered}                                                       \\tag{30}\\label{eq:30}$$ In the far cells specified by the last two lines, all individual eligibility indicators and eligible counts agree as well.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-path-lists",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **fixed interaction class** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Let $v:\mathbb R^3\to[0,\infty)$ be bounded, measurable, radial, and supported in a ball of finite radius, with $v$ not zero almost everywhere.
- **one-particle density matrix and constant orbital** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Write $\gamma^{(1)}_{N,L,T}$ for its one-particle density matrix, normalized to trace $N$, and put $u_0=V^{-1/2}$.
- **condensate fraction** ([source](#pm-h-4)): Let $q_0=\langle u_0,\gamma^{(1)}u_0\rangle/N$.
- **regional particle count** ([source](#sec:setup)): For a function $p$ of position put $n_p(X)=\sum_i p(x_i)$ on configurations (also the multiplication operator); similarly $n_U$ for counting particles in $U$.
- **von Neumann entropy** ([source](#sec:local-energy)): Here $S(\sigma)=-\operatorname{Tr}(\sigma\log\sigma)$ is the von Neumann entropy, with $0\log0=0$.
- **soft regional cutoff** ([source](#sec:localization)): $$ After summing in an eigenbasis, $\operatorname{Tr}H\sigma=\operatorname{Tr}H\tau+ \mathbb E_g\sum_i\|\nabla_iG\|^2$. Both energies and entropies are finite, since $g$ and its gradients are bounded at fixed $N,L$. To compare relative entropies, approximate $g^2$ uniformly by positive simple functions on finite partitions into symmetric measurable sets $E$. Set $p_E=\operatorname{Tr}\Gamma\mathbf1_E$ and, for $p_E>0$, $\tau_E=\Gamma^{1/2}\mathbf1_E\Gamma^{1/2}/p_E$. The unmodified and tilted states are mixtures of the same $\tau_E$, with weights $p_E$ and $p_Eg_E^2/M_G$. Monotonicity of relative entropy under discarding the classical label (a partial trace; see (Lieb and Ruskai 1973, Corollary (3.2) and Section IV) [[LiebRuskai1973Entropy](#ref-LiebRuskai1973Entropy)]) therefore bounds $D(\tau\Vert\Gamma)$ for this approximation by the classical relative entropy of these weights. Uniform approximation gives trace-norm convergence of the states and convergence of the classical entropies, since $g$ is bounded above and away from zero. Lower semicontinuity then gives $D(\tau\Vert\Gamma)\leq2\mathbb E_gG-\log M_G$. Finally $\mathcal F(\tau)-F_N=TD(\tau\Vert\Gamma)$ proves (8). ◻ </details> For a nonempty union $U$ of coarse cells, set $\phi_U(x)=e^{-d(x,U)/s}$, where distance is the Euclidean torus distance.
- **nonisolated particle** ([source](#stmt-lem-isolation)): A particle is nonisolated if another particle is within torus distance $r$.
- **eligible closed slot** ([source](#sec:slab)): A closed slot $i$ is *eligible* if all of the following hold: its start $S_i$ and end $Y_i$ are each at distance greater than $r$ from the other points of the respective tuple; its lifted path stays within $Ds$ of its end; in the first and last time intervals of length $r$ it stays within $r/10$ of its respective start and end; and both $\mathcal A^-(S_i;\omega)$ and $\mathcal A^+(Y_i;\omega)$ are at most a fixed threshold $C_0$.
- **usable cell** ([source](#sec:slab)): A cell is *usable* if its end count belongs to $[b_-m,b_+m]$ and at least a proportion $1-\varepsilon$ of those slots are eligible.
- **bridge cut** ([source](#sec:slab)): For an individual bridge, $\mathrm{cut}_R$ denotes just the lifted macro condition with radius $Rs$ and the same two interval conditions with radius $r/10$.
- **defective cell** ([source](#sec:defects)): A cell is *defective* if its end count is below $2b_-m$ or above $b_+m/2$, or if at least $\varepsilon/4$ of its slots fail eligibility with score threshold $C_0-\Delta_A$ in place of $C_0$.
- **symmetric slab event** ([source](#sec:defects)): An event on the actual slab paths is *symmetric* when it is invariant under simultaneous relabeling of all slots; it must not single out the opened slot.
- **bad-component fill** ([source](#pm-h-16)): Define its *fill* by adjoining the finite nearest-neighbor components of its lattice complement, and project the result back to the torus.
- **feasible fixed data and witness** ([source](#pm-h-28)): Call the fixed data *feasible* if some choice of the remaining original paths satisfies both nonzero selection weights, their specified adaptations, and the cuts. Call such a choice a *witness*.
- **common far-choice product** ([source](#pm-h-32)): Let $H_F(B)$ be the product of these factors for the selected far occurrences of both paths.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-condensate-lower-bound | constant-orbital condensate fraction | ≥ | $c_*(v)$ | The lower bound depends on the potential; the required volume threshold may also depend on the fixed density. |
| q-temperature-window | temperature | ≤ | $\rho^2$ | Density is fixed before the volume limit. |
| q-free-energy-forward-increment | forward free-energy increment | ≥ | $c_v\rho k$ | The constant depends on the fixed potential and is uniform over the admitted temperatures. |
| q-attractive-field-increment | single-particle free-energy increment in an attractive external field | ≤ | $C_v\rho$ | The bound is independent of the size of the bounded external field. |
| q-occupation-joint-tail | probability of simultaneous occupation shortages or excesses | ≤ | $C e^{-cmu}$ | Constants and the required coarse scale depend only on the potential. |
| q-isolation-distance | endpoint isolation distance | = | $\zeta\rho^{-1/3}$ | The isolation parameter is fixed before density is reduced. |
| q-bridge-middle-interaction | expected interaction of a probability bridge with a fixed trajectory on the middle time interval | ≤ | $C_v/\sqrt r$ | Cutoffs are fixed before the small-density and large-volume restrictions. |
| q-skeleton-encounter-exponent | exponential encounter parameter | = | $\frac{c_{\rm hit}}{(1+R_*)^2}$ | The absolute numerator is independent of torus size and admissible endpoints. |
| q-detour-component-budget | detour vertices contributed by a bad component | ≤ | $6\|T\|$ | The bound applies componentwise before loop erasure, which can only remove vertices. |
| q-path-list-cardinality | number of listed path pairs | ≤ | $4^{b_{\rm tot}}$ | The total bridge-component size controls the number of bad-subset guesses. |
| q-listed-path-encounters | near encounters of a listed path pair | ≤ | $J_0+C(R_*)b_{\rm tot}$ | The extra encounter cost depends on the fixed encounter radius and bridge-component size. |
| q-open-proposal-second-moment | likelihood-ratio second moment | ≤ | $C_v$ | The bound is uniform over the admitted density band and positive temperatures. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-neumann-energy** [claim](#stmt-lem-neumann-cell) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-neumann-cell): Use a positive-measure radial interaction set and Sobolev–Poincaré control, then pair particles under Neumann bracketing. -> Cell energy lower bounds and a particle-count multiplication bound.
- **step-entropy-control** [claim](#stmt-lem-kinetic-entropy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-kinetic-entropy): Bound an ideal Bose grand partition function by the periodic heat kernel and apply the Gibbs variational inequality. -> Kinetic entropy inequality and finiteness of entropy.
- **step-free-energy-increments** [claim](#stmt-prop-increments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-increments): Combine cell energy and entropy bounds, use entropy concavity of marginals, and insert a uniform free loop for the upper increment. -> Lower free-energy increments and an upper insertion cost uniform in bounded attractive fields.
- **step-localization-tilt** [claim](#stmt-lem-localization-tilt) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-localization-tilt): Dephase the spatial coloring into number sectors and compare the tilted state's relative entropy through a classical position partition. -> Localization and diagonal-tilt free-energy inequalities.
- **step-local-density** [claim](#stmt-lem-density-tilts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-density-tilts): Average localized supports with an exponential weight, absorb gradient errors, and integrate a tilted logarithmic moment derivative. -> Local mean density, exponential density moments, and cell count second moments.
- **step-occupation-tails** [claim](#stmt-lem-occupation-tails) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-occupation-tails): Place extra particles in a separated orbital for the shortage cost; use positive and negative density tilts for the two tails. -> Simultaneous shortage and excess estimates for specified cells.
- **step-isolation** [claim](#stmt-lem-isolation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-isolation): Count close-particle clusters using overlapping fine cubes and pay for the count with reserved interaction energy under a tilt. -> Exponential nonisolation tail.
- **step-single-bridge** [claim](#stmt-lem-single-bridge) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-single-bridge): Use nearest lifts, Brownian scaling, Gaussian density decay, and endpoint-time reversal. -> Positive cut probability and interaction-score controls.
- **step-sandwich-trace** [claim](#stmt-lem-operator-sandwich) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-operator-sandwich): Interpolate the sandwich, estimate quadratic forms, and apply exterior powers followed by an integral identity. -> A heat-trace upper bound for every positive sandwich power.
- **step-joint-trajectory-moment** [claim](#stmt-prop-atypical-moment) / [proof](#pm-h-12): Bin failures, dominate marked kernels by confined colored kernels, normalize the embedding, and estimate the resulting heat trace by local density and particle-removal costs. -> A localized joint exponential moment for interacting trajectory failures.
- **step-anchor-scores** [claim](#stmt-lem-anchor-scores) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-anchor-scores): Pack separated anchors, sum Gaussian shells, and apply Jensen with stationary one-time density moments. -> Pathwise score sum and its exponential moment.
- **step-defective-cells** [claim](#stmt-prop-defective-cells) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-defective-cells): Classify the first failed eligibility test and combine occupation, isolation, trajectory, and score tails over specified subcollections. -> A vanishing joint defect parameter per specified cell.
- **step-insertion-transfer** [claim](#stmt-prop-insertion-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-insertion-comparison): Apply Hilbert–Schmidt Cauchy–Schwarz, positivity of kernels, and the local count second moment. -> Closed-to-open event transfer with the cell-pair mass normalization.
- **step-skeleton-moments** [claim](#stmt-lem-skeleton) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-skeleton): Expose dyadic velocity variables, extract an unused block for a conditional density bound, and sum ordered encounter products. -> Thickened and soft-distance exponential encounter bounds.
- **step-detour-construction** [claim](#stmt-lem-detour) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-detour): Fill small bad components, establish star connectivity of their exterior boundaries, replace last-visit segments, and erase loops. -> Simple good-cell path with a componentwise detour budget and deletion invariance.
- **step-replacement-stability** [claim](#stmt-lem-replacement-stability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-replacement-stability): Bound changed score contributions using confinement and simple-path cell counts, then compare stricter and ordinary eligibility thresholds. -> Containment of witness bad sets and exact far selection agreement.
- **step-path-lists** [claim](#stmt-lem-path-lists) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-path-lists): Guess bad subsets only on bridge components and use deletion invariance on nonbridge components. -> Lists containing every witness path pair and an encounter bound.
- **step-integrated-cross** [claim](#stmt-lem-cross) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cross): Cancel nearest-lift kernels away from shared labels, bound proposal normalizers, restore baseline interactions on feasible fibers, and retain common far selection factors. -> Integrated reverse-insertion comparison with only shared-label and encounter costs.
- **step-second-moment** [claim](#stmt-prop-second-moment) / [proof](#pm-h-36): Sum labels and listed cell paths, recover the reverse insertion, and control rare bridge components through the soft-distance skeleton moment. -> Uniform likelihood-ratio second moment.
- **step-condensation-and-limits** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cross-2): Combine accepted proposal mass and the second moment by Cauchy–Schwarz, fix cutoffs before density and volume, and pass through trace-norm ground-state convergence to the thermodynamic lower limit. -> Uniform constant-orbital condensation at positive temperature and in the unique ground state.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-uniform-condensate**: The constant orbital has a positive condensate fraction throughout the admitted dilute density and temperature ranges, with a density-dependent volume threshold and a potential-dependent lower bound.
- **invariant-label-choice-sums**: At an ordinary common cell, coincident label choices receive the shared-label weight and distinct choices receive unit weight. At the first cell the labels must coincide; at the last cell a label of the first tuple is fixed. The normalized sums are the respective expressions in the cellwise table.
- **invariant-selection-preservation**: The stricter baseline bad-cell test contains the witness bad cells under every cut replacement, and far eligibility indicators and eligible counts agree exactly.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-finite-check-as-condensation-proof**: Treat the finite label calculation as a certificate of the uniform condensate theorem. -> The finite sum verifies only label bookkeeping; it does not establish interacting path estimates, rare-component moments, or either limiting argument.
- **anti-independent-route-selection**: Discard configuration-dependent eligibility weights before comparing the transformed path integrals. -> Changing trajectories changes eligibility and usable cells, so the selection factors cannot be removed without the replacement-stability argument.
- **anti-independent-labels-at-first-cell**: Sum the first-cell labels as independent choices. -> Independent choices include forbidden distinct-label pairs and change the endpoint normalization.
- **anti-temperature-extrapolation**: Extend the uniform condensate guarantee to temperatures outside the stated interval. -> The analytic estimates establish uniformity only in the admitted temperature regime; behavior outside it remains unproved here.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The finite construction checked here is the cellwise label enumeration in the source's label-choice table. It checks exact rational specializations of the shared-label weight, not the continuum condensate theorem. The source states: "Simplicity makes these sums independent across cells, since labels in different outer cells cannot coincide." The shared-label weight is supplied as a rational harness parameter, representing the table's exponential weight; the proof's unspecified constant is not computed.

```python
# INPUTS: A JSON object with integer n and rational string weight.
# PRECONDITIONS: 1 <= n <= 64; weight is a rational number at least 1.
# OUTPUTS: Exact rational strings for ordinary, first, and last cell sums.
# TIME: Quadratic in n in rational arithmetic operations.
# SPACE: A fixed number of rational accumulators, excluding integer bit storage.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact rational arithmetic using fractions.Fraction.
# FAILURE: Invalid input exits with nonzero status.
import json
import sys
from fractions import Fraction

def main():
    data = json.load(sys.stdin)
    if not isinstance(data, dict) or set(data) != {'n', 'weight'}:
        raise ValueError('invalid input object')
    n = data['n']
    if type(n) is not int or not 1 <= n <= 64:
        raise ValueError('invalid label count')
    if not isinstance(data['weight'], str):
        raise ValueError('weight must be a rational string')
    weight = Fraction(data['weight'])
    if weight < 1:
        raise ValueError('invalid shared-label weight')
    ordinary = Fraction(0)
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            ordinary += weight if i == j else 1
    ordinary /= n*n
    first = Fraction(0)
    for i in range(n):
        first += weight
    first /= n*n
    last = Fraction(0)
    fixed_label = 0
    for j in range(n):
        last += weight if fixed_label == j else 1
    last /= n*n
    print(json.dumps({'ordinary': str(ordinary), 'first': str(first), 'last': str(last)}, sort_keys=True))

if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()
```

    "oracle_code": "import json\nimport sys\nfrom fractions import Fraction\n\ndef main():\n    try:\n        with open(sys.argv[1], encoding='utf-8') as handle:\n            data = json.load(handle)\n        n = data['n']\n        if type(n) is not int or not 1 <= n <= 64:\n            return 1\n        if not isinstance(data['weight'], str):\n            return 1\n        weight = Fraction(data['weight'])\n        if weight < 1:\n            return 1\n        # Closed forms derived by counting diagonal and off-diagonal pairs.\n        diagonal_excess = weight - 1\n        truth = {\n            'ordinary': 1 + diagonal_excess / n,\n            'first': weight / n,\n            'last': (1 + diagonal_excess / n) / n,\n        }\n        with open(sys.argv[2], encoding='utf-8') as handle:\n            candidate = json.load(handle)\n        expected = json.loads(sys.argv[3])\n        if not isinstance(candidate, dict) or not isinstance(expected, dict):\n            return 1\n        if set(candidate) != set(truth) or set(expected) != set(truth):\n            return 1\n        for key, value in truth.items():\n            if not isinstance(candidate[key], str) or not isinstance(expected[key], str):\n                return 1\n            if Fraction(candidate[key]) != value or Fraction(expected[key]) != value:\n                return 1\n        return 0\n    except Exception:\n        return 1\n\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n    sys.exit(main())\n",

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The equilibrium-state result supplies no software correctness contract or application architecture. Its finite geometric detour construction is assessed separately under algorithms. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The imaginary-time bridge estimates concern equilibrium quantum path measures. They provide no real-time simulation update rule, collision response, or numerical error control. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The insertion comparison bounds a finite measure of symmetric slab events; it does not construct an executable analysis tool or specify its inputs and diagnostics. |
| algorithms | True | strong | implementable | inferred | The deterministic detour procedure constructs a simple route through good cells on the periodic grid from an accepted nearest-neighbor skeleton. This finite geometric subroutine can be implemented independently of the interacting gas. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The finite path lists support a comparison proof, but their cardinality bound supplies no storage representation, indexing method, or operation-cost contract for a general data structure. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The trace inequality controls operators on enlarged particle Hilbert spaces. It specifies no accelerator architecture, arithmetic implementation, or parallel execution schedule. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The random skeleton construction proves encounter-moment bounds for a specified geometric law. It supplies no adversary model or cryptographic unpredictability guarantee. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The localization and tilting estimates concern symmetric quantum states and their free energies. No learning objective, training procedure, or generalization guarantee is constructed. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The particle-number free-energy inequalities constrain the physical ensemble. They do not provide a finite optimisation formulation, a solver, or an approximation guarantee for an operational objective. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The joint defective-cell estimate is derived for the interacting closed path law. A distributed failure process has no established identification with that law, and the result supplies no communication or consensus protocol. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The direct scope is mathematical analysis of Bose–Einstein condensation at fixed interaction and density. The main theorem proves existence of uniform positive bounds rather than a finite procedure computing the condensate fraction or its thresholds. |

          "exact_quote": "For the independent skeletons just constructed, there are absolute constants $c_{\\mathrm{hit}}>0$ and $C<\\infty$ such that, for every $R_*\\ge1$, $$\\mathbb E e^{\\alpha J_0}\\le C,\\qquad\n             \\alpha=\\frac{c_{\\rm hit}}{(1+R_*)^2}.\n             \\tag{24}\\label{eq:24}$$ For every fixed $B>0$ there are $\\xi(B)>0$ and $C(B)<\\infty$ such that $$\\mathbb E e^{\\xi(B)S_B}\\le C(B).\n             \\tag{25}\\label{eq:25}$$ Both estimates are uniform in $n$ and in every admissible ordered pair.",

        }

Then $$\\mathcal F^{(-W)}(\\sigma)+\\operatorname{Tr}\n n_{|\\nabla p|^2+|\\nabla q|^2}\\sigma\n \\geq\\sum_{j=0}^Jw_j\\bigl[\n \\mathcal F^{(-W)}(\\sigma_{\\mathrm{in},j})+\n \\mathcal F^{(-W)}(\\sigma_{\\mathrm{out},j})\\bigr]-TH(w).\n \\tag{7}\\label{eq:7}$$ Here $H(w)=-\\sum_jw_j\\log w_j$, with $0\\log0=0$, is the Shannon entropy, and $H(w)\\leq C+\\sum_jjw_j$ and $\\sum_jjw_j=\\operatorname{Tr}n_{p^2}\\sigma$.*\n\n*Let $\\Gamma=Z_N^{-1}e^{-\\beta H_{N,L}}$, let $P$ be its position law, and let $G$ be a bounded symmetric Lipschitz real function of positions. <a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-fixed-potential-class** (hypothesis): The interaction must be bounded, measurable, nonnegative, radial, supported in a finite-radius ball, and not zero almost everywhere. The result does not cover all repulsive interactions. Blocked use: ['Applying the theorem to attractive or sign-changing pair potentials.', 'Applying the theorem directly to hard-core interactions, nonradial interactions, or interactions without finite range.']
- **limit-dilute-temperature-volume-window** (hypothesis): The uniform lower bound requires an admitted positive reference density, the stated particle-density band, the admitted temperature interval, and a sufficiently large volume at that density. The theorem gives no conclusion outside these conditions. Blocked use: ['Claiming condensation at arbitrary density or temperature.', 'Applying the asymptotic guarantee to a finite volume without establishing its threshold.']
- **limit-fixed-interaction-limit-order** (formal_scope): The physical potential and a positive density remain fixed before the thermodynamic limit. The volume threshold may depend on density even though the lower condensate fraction does not. Blocked use: ['Replacing the fixed interaction by a particle-number-dependent scaling and treating the conclusion as unchanged.', 'Assuming a common volume threshold throughout the dilute density interval.']
- **limit-positive-fraction-only** (formal_scope): The conclusion concerns a positive lower bound on occupation of the spatially constant orbital in equilibrium. It does not assert complete condensation, identify an exact condensate fraction, or establish real-time transport behavior. Blocked use: ['Reporting the lower bound as the exact condensate fraction.', 'Using the condensation theorem alone to certify superfluid transport or real-time dynamics.']
- **limit-energy-entropy-state-domains** (hypothesis): The local analytic estimates use form-domain functions and finite-energy symmetric trace-one states. The entropy estimate distinguishes Dirichlet subregions from the full periodic torus. Blocked use: ['Applying the entropy inequality to an arbitrary state without its finite-energy and symmetry assumptions.', 'Transferring the local estimates to unspecified boundary conditions.']
- **limit-localization-and-tilt-admissibility** (hypothesis): Spatial localization requires the stated color identity and form-domain mapping. Diagonal tilting requires a bounded symmetric Lipschitz real position function; the localized density estimate further restricts the external field. Blocked use: ['Using discontinuous or unbounded tilts without a separate approximation argument.', 'Applying the localized density bound to an arbitrary external field merely because the upper free-energy increment permits a bounded field of unrestricted size.']
- **limit-joint-tails-not-independence** (formal_scope): The occupation and defect estimates concern specified cell collections under the stated equilibrium laws. Joint upper bounds do not establish independence of cell failures or independence of interacting trajectories. Blocked use: ['Replacing the interacting bridge law by independent bridges when estimating simultaneous failures.', 'Treating the defect parameter as an independent failure probability for arbitrary cells or external systems.']
- **limit-lift-cut-and-scale-conditions** (hypothesis): Single-bridge estimates require the endpoint displacement restriction and a sufficiently large torus. Lifted confinement and short endpoint-time restraints are part of the bridge cut; a nearest spatial representative selected separately at each time is not the specified lift. Blocked use: ['Applying the nearest-lift estimate to arbitrary endpoint displacements or insufficiently large tori.', 'Checking confinement after changing winding representatives along a trajectory.', 'Dropping endpoint isolation or short-time restraints while retaining the small distinct-bridge interaction cost.']
- **limit-cutoff-hierarchy** (hypothesis): The cutoffs are fixed in the prescribed order before reducing density, and the volume threshold is selected afterward at each fixed density. Uniformity does not authorize independent or reversed choices of these parameters. Blocked use: ['Allowing comparison constants to vary with density while claiming the same uniform lower bound.', 'Choosing a trajectory cutoff without checking the subsequent isolation, score, and encounter restrictions.']
- **limit-symmetric-open-events** (hypothesis): The closed-to-open insertion comparison applies to measurable symmetric slab events and uses actual inner endpoints. The insertion measure is a finite measure, so its event mass must not be interpreted as a normalized probability without further normalization. Blocked use: ['Applying the transfer inequality to an event that singles out the opened slot.', 'Substituting outer endpoints for actual inner endpoints in the symmetric defective test.', 'Reading an insertion event mass directly as a conditional probability.']
- **limit-skeleton-law-and-geometry** (hypothesis): The encounter bounds apply to independent skeletons from the prescribed dyadic construction and admissible ordered endpoints on the periodic cubic grid. They are not universal bounds for arbitrary random routes or arbitrary graph topologies. Blocked use: ['Replacing the skeleton law by an arbitrary walk while keeping the encounter constants.', 'Applying the geometric guarantee to a directed dependency graph or a network without the required lattice realization.']
- **limit-detour-rejection-and-deletion** (hypothesis): The deterministic detour construction is conditional on geometric acceptance. It uses the prescribed component-size and endpoint-fill rejection rules and permits star steps in the output. Its deletion invariance concerns whole bad components not hit by the accepted skeleton. Blocked use: ['Demanding a route after a rejection condition is met.', 'Claiming that the output retains nearest-neighbor steps or minimizes route length.', 'Replacing whole-component deletion by arbitrary deletion of bad cells.']
- **limit-normalized-interacting-proposal** (hypothesis): The opening construction uses the conditional interacting distribution restricted by the bridge cuts, with its normalization retained. Positivity of that normalization is established through the admitted displacement conditions. Blocked use: ['Replacing the proposal by independent free bridges without a new comparison proof.', 'Omitting proposal normalizers from the likelihood ratio.', 'Assigning positive proposal weight when its endpoint and eligibility conditions have not been established.']
- **limit-feasible-fibers-and-selection** (hypothesis): The cross comparison depends on feasible original witnesses, stability under every baseline cut replacement, and retention of the common far-choice product. The path-list predicate is part of the constrained baseline integral. Blocked use: ['Removing configuration-dependent choice weights before establishing their agreement under replacements.', 'Selecting only favorable replacements instead of proving stability for every admitted replacement.', 'Treating candidate routes as independent of the gas configuration.']
- **limit-finite-label-certificate-scope** (formal_scope): The cellwise label calculation verifies finite counting identities conditional on the admitted path pair and occupancy assumptions. It does not certify the continuum interaction estimates, comparison mass, uniform second moment, or limiting arguments. Blocked use: ['Promoting agreement of a finite label enumeration with its counting formula into a certificate of the condensation theorem.', 'Using rational specializations of the shared-label weight as computed values of the analytic comparison constant.']
- **limit-uncomputed-thresholds** (unknown): Explicit numerical values of the potential-dependent density threshold, volume threshold, and condensate lower bound are ABSENT. The proof specifies a hierarchy of sufficiently large and sufficiently small choices rather than a numerical evaluation procedure. Blocked use: ['Publishing a numerical threshold or condensate prediction as a computed consequence of the theorem.', 'Treating existence of uniform constants as an executable membership test for a supplied physical instance.']
- **limit-zero-temperature-passage** (formal_scope): The ground-state conclusion is obtained by taking the zero-temperature limit at fixed admitted particle number and volume, using positivity improvement, uniqueness, and trace-norm Gibbs convergence. It does not independently justify exchanging arbitrary joint temperature and volume limits. Blocked use: ['Replacing the stated ground-state passage by an unproved interchange of limits.', 'Extending the conclusion to arbitrary nonequilibrium low-energy states.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


For each fixed bounded measurable, nonnegative, radial interaction of finite range in three dimensions that is not zero almost everywhere, we prove a positive lower bound on the constant-orbital condensate fraction that is uniform over all sufficiently small densities and all temperatures between zero and the square of the density. The interaction and density remain fixed in the thermodynamic limit.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### The model and the uniform bound

For a homogeneous gas, Bose–Einstein condensation means that one orbital contains a positive fraction of all particles in the equilibrium state. We consider the spatially constant orbital and keep both the physical interaction and a positive density fixed as the volume grows. The bound below uses the same positive fraction throughout a sufficiently dilute interval of densities.

Let $v:\mathbb R^3\to[0,\infty)$ be bounded, measurable, radial, and supported in a ball of finite radius, with $v$ not zero almost everywhere. On the torus $\Lambda_L=(\mathbb R/L\mathbb Z)^3$, of volume $V=L^3$, let $v_L(x)=\sum_{k\in\mathbb Z^3}v(x+Lk)$ and define $$H_{N,L}=-\sum_{i=1}^N\Delta_i+
                \sum_{1\le i<j\le N}v_L(x_i-x_j)
 \quad\hbox{on }L^2_{\mathrm{sym}}(\Lambda_L^N).$$ The units are $\hbar^2/(2m_{\mathrm{particle}})=k_B=1$. At $T>0$, the state is the canonical Gibbs state $\Gamma_{N,L,T}=e^{-H_{N,L}/T}/\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}e^{-H_{N,L}/T}$; at $T=0$, it is the ground state. Write $\gamma^{(1)}_{N,L,T}$ for its one-particle density matrix, normalized to trace $N$, and put $u_0=V^{-1/2}$.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *For every potential $v$ as above there are constants $\rho_*(v)>0$ and $c_*(v)>0$ with the following property. For every $0<\rho<\rho_*(v)$ there is $L_0(\rho,v)<\infty$ such that, for every $L\ge L_0(\rho,v)$, every integer $N\ge1$ satisfying $\rho/2\le N/L^3\le2\rho$, and every $0\le T\le\rho^2$, $$\frac{\langle u_0,\gamma^{(1)}_{N,L,T}u_0\rangle}{N}
 \ge c_*(v).$$ Consequently, for each such fixed density $\rho$ and fixed $0\le T\le\rho^2$, every sequence with $L\to\infty$ and $N/L^3\to\rho$ has lower limiting condensate fraction at least $c_*(v)$.*

</div>

The volume threshold may depend on the density; the condensate lower bound does not. The Hamiltonian is the exact interacting Hamiltonian, and its potential is not rescaled with the number of particles. The conclusion is macroscopic occupation of the constant orbital with a positive lower bound throughout the admitted dilute interval. The zero-temperature state is unambiguous: positivity of the finite-volume heat kernel gives a unique bosonic ground state, as shown at the end of the proof.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Context and antecedents

The ideal-gas theory begins with Bose’s counting of light quanta (Bose 1924) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Bose1924](#ref-Bose1924)] and Einstein’s extension to a monatomic ideal gas, including the condensation prediction in his second paper (Einstein 1924, 1925) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Einstein1924IdealGas](#ref-Einstein1924IdealGas), [Einstein1925IdealGas](#ref-Einstein1925IdealGas)]. Bogoliubov’s treatment of weakly interacting bosons introduced an approximate quasiparticle description based on macroscopic occupation of one mode (Bogolubov 1947) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Bogolubov1947](#ref-Bogolubov1947)].

For interacting particles, Penrose and Onsager formulated condensation through a macroscopic eigenvalue of the one-particle density matrix and applied this viewpoint to liquid helium (Penrose and Onsager 1956) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[PenroseOnsager1956](#ref-PenroseOnsager1956)]. Yang developed the broader framework of off-diagonal long-range order, covering Bose condensation and fermion pairing (Yang 1962) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Yang1962ODLRO](#ref-Yang1962ODLRO)]. These criteria identify the order to be proved; establishing it for a specified interacting Hamiltonian remains a separate mathematical problem.

Rigorous lattice antecedents include Dyson, Lieb, and Simon’s low-temperature long-range order for the spin-$\tfrac12$ nearest-neighbor quantum XY model on the simple cubic lattice in dimensions $d\ge3$ (Dyson, Lieb, and Simon 1978) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[DysonLiebSimon1978](#ref-DysonLiebSimon1978)], and Kennedy, Lieb, and Shastry’s ground-state long-range order for all spins on hypercubic lattices in dimensions $d\ge2$ (Kennedy, Lieb, and Shastry 1988) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[KennedyLiebShastry1988XY](#ref-KennedyLiebShastry1988XY)]. For spin-$\tfrac12$, the spin–hard-core-boson correspondence relates these results to condensation of half-filled lattice bosons. Passing to a continuum Hamiltonian with a fixed interaction and a prescribed density requires additional arguments.

The distinction between the thermodynamic and Gross–Pitaevskii limits is essential. Lieb and Seiringer proved complete condensation in the Gross–Pitaevskii limit, with the interaction scaled so that $Na$ remains fixed, where $a$ is the scattering length (Lieb and Seiringer 2002) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[LiebSeiringer2002BEC](#ref-LiebSeiringer2002BEC)]. Deuchert and Seiringer subsequently treated positive-temperature homogeneous gases with scattering length $a_N=a_vL/N$, where $a_v$ is the scattering length of the unscaled potential, and controlled the one-particle density matrix of approximate Gibbs minimizers (Deuchert and Seiringer 2020) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[DeuchertSeiringer2020GP](#ref-DeuchertSeiringer2020GP)]. These results allow the interaction scale to vary with particle number. The problem considered here instead fixes the potential and a positive density before taking the volume to infinity.

The limits between Gross–Pitaevskii scaling and a fixed-density thermodynamic limit have several forms. Brennecke, Brooks, Caraci, and Oldenburg proved complete ground-state condensation in the Gross–Pitaevskii regime and beyond (Brennecke et al. 2025) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[BrenneckeBrooksCaraciOldenburg2025](#ref-BrenneckeBrooksCaraciOldenburg2025)]. Fournais treated low-energy states on growing boxes in a joint dilute and increasing-volume limit (Søren Fournais 2021, Theorem 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Fournais2021](#ref-Fournais2021)]. Chong, Liang, and Nam gave simpler proofs of low-energy condensation using kinetic localization and Poincaré-type inequalities (Chong, Liang, and Nam 2026) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[ChongLiangNam2026](#ref-ChongLiangNam2026)]. For the unit-box interaction $N^{2-2\kappa}v(N^{1-\kappa}x)$, rescaling to a fixed potential gives density $N^{3\kappa-2}$; the regimes $\kappa<2/3$ therefore couple dilution to increasing particle number. Junge’s low-temperature canonical Gibbs-state result uses Neumann boxes whose size remains coupled to the dilute limit (Junge 2026, Corollary 6) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Junge2026Propagation](#ref-Junge2026Propagation)].

The thermodynamic energy problem has a separate history. Dyson’s hard-sphere bounds and the leading dilute asymptotics of Lieb and Yngvason established the first-order energy scale (Dyson 1957; Lieb and Yngvason 1998) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Dyson1957](#ref-Dyson1957), [LiebYngvason1998](#ref-LiebYngvason1998)]. The next correction was predicted by Lee, Huang, and Yang’s pseudopotential calculation (Lee, Huang, and Yang 1957) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[LeeHuangYang1957](#ref-LeeHuangYang1957)]. Fournais and Solovej proved the corresponding lower bound for nonnegative radial finite-range potentials, including hard cores (Søren Fournais and Solovej 2023, Theorems 1.1 and 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[FournaisSolovej2023](#ref-FournaisSolovej2023)]; recent upper bounds attain the same coefficient for hard spheres and for general nonnegative even finite-range potentials (Basti et al. 2026b, 2026a) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[BastiEtAl2026HardSpheres](#ref-BastiEtAl2026HardSpheres), [BastiEtAl2026GeneralPotentials](#ref-BastiEtAl2026GeneralPotentials)]. These energy results, and low-temperature free-energy estimates for strong potentials (S. Fournais et al. 2026) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[FournaisEtAl2026StrongFreeEnergy](#ref-FournaisEtAl2026StrongFreeEnergy)], do not by themselves determine occupation of a single orbital: the one-particle kinetic gap tends to zero as the volume grows.

A distinct thermodynamic-limit result appears in Sütő’s two-step preprint (Sütő 2023) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[Suto2023TwoStep](#ref-Suto2023TwoStep)]. Its Theorem 1.2 assumes nonnegativity both of the potential and of its Fourier transform, together with integrability, a Fourier-moment condition, and spatial decay, and states condensation above a temperature-dependent density threshold. Fourier positivity is an additional restriction: for example, a nonnegative spherical square barrier has a Fourier transform that changes sign. That result therefore does not cover all bounded nonnegative radial finite-range potentials, and its fixed-temperature threshold does not alone provide the joint low-density and low-temperature uniformity sought here.

Galanda and Pinamonti construct algebraic equilibrium states with a condensate background under quantitative convergence conditions for a spatially localized interaction (Galanda and Pinamonti 2025, Theorem 6.8) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[GalandaPinamonti2025Equilibrium](#ref-GalandaPinamonti2025Equilibrium)]. Their subsequent analysis treats renormalized correlation functions in Gross–Pitaevskii scaling, with a zero-temperature limit in its final convergence theorem (Galanda and Pinamonti 2026, Theorem 7.3) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[GalandaPinamonti2026GP](#ref-GalandaPinamonti2026GP)]. These constructions use a different state formulation and coupled interaction, regulator, and temperature limits from the fixed-potential canonical thermodynamic limit considered here.

The trajectory formulation descends from Feynman’s treatment of the Bose gas (Feynman 1953) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Feynman1953AtomicLambda](#ref-Feynman1953AtomicLambda)]; for the canonical Feynman–Kac representation and its symmetrization and one-particle normalizations, see (Ueltschi 2006, Appendix A) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[Ueltschi2006FeynmanCycles](#ref-Ueltschi2006FeynmanCycles)]. The random-path comparison has a different ancestry. Benjamini, Pemantle, and Peres constructed paths with exponentially decaying intersection tails (Benjamini, Pemantle, and Peres 1998) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[BenjaminiPemantlePeres1998](#ref-BenjaminiPemantlePeres1998)]. Häggström and Mossel developed a hierarchical random-drift construction with low predictability; its independent block variables are a close antecedent of the dyadic velocities used below (Häggström and Mossel 1998, sec. 3, Proposition 3.1) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[HaggstromMossel1998](#ref-HaggstromMossel1998)]. Abbe, Massoulié, Montanari, Sly, and Srivastava used finite-endpoint path averages and overlap estimates for group synchronization (Abbe et al. 2018) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[AbbeMassoulieMontanariSlySrivastava2018](#ref-AbbeMassoulieMontanariSlySrivastava2018)]; Garban and Spencer used this approach for classical disordered-spin symmetry breaking along the Nishimori line (Garban and Spencer 2023) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[GarbanSpencer2023](#ref-GarbanSpencer2023)]. Here the finite cell paths, their thickened intersection estimates, and the continuum bridge comparison require separate proofs.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### How the comparison works

Let $q_0=\langle u_0,\gamma^{(1)}u_0\rangle/N$. The Feynman–Kac formula expresses the partition function by positive weights on closed collections of paths. Replacing one matching endpoint by an independently integrated spatial point gives a positive measure $\mu$ of total mass $q_0$. We construct a measure $\nu$ by changing a short portion of closed paths, with mass at least a fixed $a>0$, and show that its density $f=\,\mathrm d\nu/\,\mathrm d\mu$ has bounded second moment. Cauchy–Schwarz then gives $$a^2\le q_0\int f^2\,\mathrm d\mu.$$ Thus the main problem is a comparison of positive path measures.

Partition space into cells of side $s$ comparable to $\rho^{-1/2}$, and change paths only during a time interval of length $\delta=s^2$. The mean count $m=Ns^3/V$ is comparable to $\rho^{-1/2}$, making many particle choices available in a typical cell as the density decreases, while the slab remains short compared with $1/T$ throughout the positive-temperature range.

The local analysis must control simultaneous failures in specified families of cells. Energy and entropy estimates first give occupation tails and separation of most endpoints at the shorter scale $r=\zeta\rho^{-1/3}$, with $\zeta>0$ fixed and small. A particle is eligible when its endpoints are separated, its path stays near them during short initial and final time intervals, and it satisfies overall confinement and integrated interaction-score bounds for the path ensemble. The interacting bridges are not independent. A separate localized partition-function comparison bounds their joint trajectory failures, with a cost proportional to the volume of the tested cells. Together with the one-time estimates and score bounds, this makes specified sets of unusable cells unlikely.

Between two distant cells, choose a random lattice path, called a skeleton, whose encounters with an independent copy have bounded exponential moments. Here an encounter records a vertex within a fixed cell distance of the other path; a second estimate weights all vertices by exponentially decaying distance. These two geometric bounds are established independently of the gas in Section <a href="#sec:skeletons" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:skeletons">8.1</a>. Detour around unusable components. Select one eligible particle from each cell on the resulting simple path, and cyclically reassign their starting points. One end is opened and the selected bridges are sampled from their exactly normalized interacting law. This defines $\nu$.

The second moment involves two independently chosen paths in related configurations. Estimating both changes against the original closed configuration would incur a cost proportional to their lengths. Instead, a reverse insertion supplies a baseline in which contributions far from encounters cancel. Two effects remain small as $\rho$ decreases: an encounter of distinct separated bridges has interaction cost tending to zero at rate $r^{-1/2}$, and coincident particle choices have probability of order $1/m$.

Changing trajectories also changes which cells are usable and which particles are eligible, so the two routes cannot be treated as independent of the gas. A stricter cell test in the reverse configuration controls these changes: away from encounters the selection weights agree, while rare components give a controlled list of possible detours. Keeping those selection weights throughout the comparison yields the required second-moment bound after averaging over the environment and the two paths.

Sections <a href="#sec:energy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:energy">[sec:energy]</a>–<a href="#sec:defects" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:defects">7</a> prove the local analytic bounds. Section <a href="#sec:geometry" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:geometry">8</a> constructs the paths and detours. Section <a href="#sec:opening" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:opening">9</a> defines the change of measure, and Section <a href="#sec:comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:comparison">10</a> proves its second-moment estimate with all normalizations and selection constraints retained. Section <a href="#sec:conclusion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:conclusion">11</a> fixes the parameters in order and passes the uniform positive-temperature bound to zero temperature.

<a id="sec:setup"></a>
### Scales and conventions

We use the Hamiltonian of Section <a href="#sec:introduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:introduction">1</a> also on labelled coordinates, and suppress $L$ when it is fixed. We choose an everywhere nonnegative bounded Borel radial representative of $v$ vanishing outside its range ball. Changing this representative on a null set changes neither the operator nor the path integrals: at interior times the free relative positions have densities. Write $R_v$ for a range bound, and use $\|v\|_1,\|v\|_\infty$ on $\mathbb R^3$. In formulas on the torus, $v$ abbreviates its periodization $v_L$; infinite-space norms always refer to the original potential.

The orthogonal symmetrizer is included exactly once in a trace kernel. If $k_t$ is the labelled $N$-particle kernel, then $$k_t^{\mathrm{sym}}(X,Y)=\frac1{N!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathfrak S_N}
                         k_t(X,\pi Y),\qquad
 Z_N=\int k_\beta^{\mathrm{sym}}(X,X)\,\mathrm dX.$$ Thus integration over labelled coordinates carries no further factorial. We work with $\rho/2\le N/V\le2\rho$, $0<T\le\rho^2$, and $\beta=T^{-1}$. Every volume restriction below is imposed at fixed $\rho$, uniformly over this temperature interval. Constants $C,c>0$ may change between occurrences and depend on $v$ and previously fixed cutoffs, but not on $\rho,L,N,T$. The final order of those cutoff choices is recorded in Section <a href="#sec:conclusion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:conclusion">11</a>.

We use a periodic grid $\mathcal G=(\mathbb Z/n\mathbb Z)^3$ of equal cubes (fix the assignment at faces), with $$n=\left\lfloor\frac{L}{K\rho^{-1/2}}\right\rfloor,\qquad
 s=L/n\in[K\rho^{-1/2},2K\rho^{-1/2}],\qquad
 M=n^3,\quad m=N/M .$$ Here $K\ge1$ is a fixed large constant chosen below. Thus $\rho s^3/2\le m\le 2\rho s^3$.

A union $U$ of $u\ge1$ cells has volume $|U|=u s^3$. A neighborhood out to distance $h s$, $h\ge0$, can be covered by at most $C(h+2)^3 u$ cells, including on the torus. Physical distances $d(\cdot,\cdot)$ are Euclidean torus distances unless indicated otherwise. Cell index distances will use the periodic sup norm. For a function $p$ of position put $n_p(X)=\sum_i p(x_i)$ on configurations (also the multiplication operator); similarly $n_U$ for counting particles in $U$.

<a id="sec:local-energy"></a>
### Energy and free-energy estimates

<span id="sec:energy" label="sec:energy"></span>

This section gives the particle-number costs needed to localize the gas. Throughout, $0<T\leq\rho^2$, $\beta=T^{-1}$, and $\rho/2\leq N/V\leq2\rho$. All statements hold after decreasing a potential-dependent upper bound on $\rho$ and increasing the volume threshold at each fixed density. The Hamiltonian with a bounded external potential $-W$ is denoted by $H_{j,L}^{(-W)}$; its symmetric partition function and free energy are $Z_j^{(-W)}$ and $F_j^{(-W)}=-T\log Z_j^{(-W)}$. We set $Z_0=1$ and $F_0=0$. For a trace-one state $\sigma$, write $\mathcal F^{(-W)}(\sigma)=\operatorname{Tr}H^{(-W)}\sigma-TS(\sigma)$. Here $S(\sigma)=-\operatorname{Tr}(\sigma\log\sigma)$ is the von Neumann entropy, with $0\log0=0$. Constants may depend on $v$, and later on already fixed dimensionless parameters, but never on $\rho,L,N,T$.

<a id="stmt-lem-neumann-cell"></a>
<div id="lem:neumann-cell" class="lemma">

**Lemma 2** (A Neumann cell bound). *There are $b_0,c_v>0$, depending only on the fixed potential, such that for a cube $Q$ of side $b\geq b_0$, every $y\in Q$, and every $f\in H^1(Q)$, <a id="eq:1"></a>$$\int_Q\bigl(|\nabla f|^2+v(x-y)|f|^2\bigr)\,dx
 \geq c_v b^{-3}\int_Q|f|^2\,dx.\tag{1}\label{eq:1}$$ The same conclusion holds for fixed positive coefficients in front of the two terms, with changed constants. Consequently, for disjoint cubes $Q$ of common side $b$, kinetic energy plus nonnegative pair energy bounds below the multiplication operator <a id="eq:2"></a>$$c_v b^{-3}\sum_Q n_Q\mathbf1_{\{n_Q\geq2\}}.\tag{2}\label{eq:2}$$ Both statements apply to torus cubes smaller than the torus, and to Dirichlet states extended by zero.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-neumann-cell"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-neumann-cell</summary>

*Proof.* Choose $\nu>0$ for which the radial set $A=\{x:v(x)\geq\nu\}$ has positive measure. Increase $b_0$ so that $b_0/2>R_v$ and $b_0\geq1$. For each $y\in Q$ choose, independently in the three coordinate directions, an orientation with available length at least $b/2$. The resulting octant of $y+A$ lies in $Q$ and has measure $|A|/8=:a_v>0$ by radial symmetry. Call it $A_y$. For $f_0=|Q|^{-1}\int_Q f$, the scale-invariant Sobolev–Poincaré inequality gives $\|f-f_0\|_6\leq C\|\nabla f\|_2$. Thus $$a_v|f_0|^2\leq2\int_{A_y}|f|^2+
 2\int_{A_y}|f-f_0|^2
 \leq2\nu^{-1}\int_Qv(x-y)|f|^2+
 C a_v^{2/3}\int_Q|\nabla f|^2.$$ Moreover $\|f-f_0\|_2^2\leq Cb^2\|\nabla f\|_2^2$. Since $b^2\leq b^3$ for $b\geq1$, these estimates give (1). Positive fixed energy coefficients only change the constants.

For (2), cut configuration space according to the cube containing each coordinate and discard cross-cube interactions. This is Neumann bracketing: the restrictions of an $H^1$ function to the pieces are admissible with no boundary condition at the cuts. Within a cube containing $j\geq2$ coordinates, pair them disjointly. For each pair apply (1) in the first coordinate with the other coordinate held fixed, and then integrate the other coordinates. No coordinate kinetic term or pair interaction is used twice. The number of pairs is $\lfloor j/2\rfloor\geq j/3$. Summing the resulting inequalities proves (2). The argument is unaffected by additional nonnegative periodic images of $v$. Zero extension gives the Dirichlet assertion. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-lem-kinetic-entropy"></a>
<div id="lem:kinetic-entropy" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3** (An entropy bound). *If $\Omega$ is an open subset of the torus with Dirichlet kinetic energy, or the full torus with periodic kinetic energy, then for every $\alpha>0$ and every finite-energy symmetric trace-one $j$-particle state, <a id="eq:3"></a>$$\alpha\operatorname{Tr}\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^j-\Delta_i\Bigr)\sigma
 -TS(\sigma)
 \geq-Tj-C_\alpha T|\Omega|(V^{-1}+T^{3/2}).\tag{3}\label{eq:3}$$ In particular a state of finite kinetic energy has finite entropy.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-kinetic-entropy"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-kinetic-entropy</summary>

*Proof.* The periodic heat kernel satisfies $K_t(0)\leq C(V^{-1}+t^{-3/2})$ by its Gaussian image sum, or equivalently by its Fourier series. Killing at $\partial\Omega$ decreases its diagonal, so $\operatorname{Tr}e^{t\alpha\Delta_\Omega}
\leq C_\alpha|\Omega|(V^{-1}+t^{-3/2})$. The ideal Bose grand partition function with fugacity $e^{-1}$ has logarithm $$\sum_{l\geq1}\frac{e^{-l}}l
 \operatorname{Tr}e^{l\beta\alpha\Delta_\Omega}
 \leq C_\alpha|\Omega|(V^{-1}+T^{3/2}).$$ Its $j$-particle term is $e^{-j}$ times the canonical partition function. Applying the Gibbs variational inequality to that term gives (3). For an arbitrary finite-energy state, the same inequality follows first on finite spectral truncations and then by monotone entropy approximation, or directly from nonnegativity of relative entropy to the ideal Gibbs state. The right side is finite, proving the entropy assertion as well. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-prop-increments"></a>
<div id="prop:increments" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:6"></a>
**Proposition 4** (Particle-number free-energy bounds). *Uniformly in the temperature range under consideration, <a id="eq:4"></a><a id="eq:5"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 F_N&\geq c_v\rho N,\tag{4}\label{eq:4}\\
 F_{N+k}&\geq F_N+c_v\rho k\qquad(k\geq0).\tag{5}\label{eq:5}
\end{aligned}$$ For every bounded $W\geq0$, with no upper bound on its size required, $$F_j^{(-W)}-F_{j-1}^{(-W)}\leq C_v\rho
